# Wordocious — Project Bible

*Last updated: 2026-08-01 (see numbered entries through §199; latest: THE STREAK POPUP THAT CRIED WOLF — the at-risk check judged a disk-cached profile, so playing on another device still triggered "Streak at Risk!" (fixed with fresh server reads on iOS/Android); plus the 142 peak-moment batch: "Streak saved!" beat, store rating prompts riding the Flawless celebration, widget deep links, and a lock-screen widget; Android 47 cut for Doug, iOS 1.3/142 cut and SUBMITTED for review, payout bank VERIFIED ($0.11); earlier: THE ADMIN WEB PORTAL NEVER WORKED — the middleware gate read an auth cookie no code ever set (sessions live in localStorage), so /admin bounced every admin including the founder, always; found only when Jasson (role=admin all along) tried the front door; fixed with a cookie mirror + refresh relay; also: the engineering portal Jasson requested is live at /portal (admin-gated) + standalone WORDOCIOUS_PORTAL.html; earlier: ANDROID CAN FINALLY TAKE MONEY — merchant account, 15% fee, payout bank all done in one morning; a founder content audit found the How to Play scoring page still describing the RETIRED V1 formula; earlier: the 6/7-letter run found the inflector would have ADDED a slur; the guess dictionary had no rules at all — it was the 1934 Webster's; 1.1 LIVE on the App Store).*

**(199) THE PORTAL LEARNS TO SEE — THIRTEEN BLIND SPOTS CLOSED IN ONE BATCH (2026-08-01, evening).** Founder asked what data we already hold that nobody can see; the brainstorm found thirteen items and he ordered all of them. The pattern that made it cheap: almost everything was ALREADY COLLECTED — tables built for one feature, instrumentation shipped for a memo, SQL written and never given a page. **NEW PAGES:** **/admin/metrics** — the Weekly Five (memo §14: DAU, D1/D7/D30 cohorts, share rate, Pro conversion, reports) finally rendered instead of living as paste-into-SQL-editor blocks in scripts/weekly-five.sql, plus share breakdowns by mode/surface, sign-in provider mix, and guest→account conversions. **/admin/puzzles** — per-day per-mode difficulty from daily_results (win rate under 40%% renders red: a brutal word is now visible before players tweet it), mode popularity, streak distribution and the shield economy. **/admin/ops** — cron heartbeats (new system_heartbeats table, upserted by all three cron routes; a stale heartbeat goes red — the difference between a quietly dead cron and knowing), the store-webhook ledger BY DAY (the ledger records successes only, so the failure signal is SILENCE, and the page says so), VS match health, and an **anti-cheat watchlist** built on §guard_precision's deliberate gap: the DB clamps solo scores at 10,000 but the legit V2 maximum is 3,072 (vs: 120,000 clamp / 105,050 real) — rows between the real max and the clamp pass the trigger and are exactly what the watchlist flags. **Payments** grew a forward-looking lifecycle strip (expiring-within-7-days, referral gift pipeline states). **NEW INSTRUMENTATION (the only parts that needed anything new):** profiles.app_version / app_platform / last_seen_at, stamped once per session by all three clients — "how many users are still on 1.1" was previously UNANSWERABLE — and profiles.converted_from_guest, stamped at the exact code line where a guest's localStorage flag is superseded by a real session. Gotcha caught in review: AGP 8 doesn't generate BuildConfig by default — Android stamps via PackageManager/PackageInfoCompat instead. All stamps are fire-and-forget and tolerate the columns not existing, because the columns didn't exist until **manual-migrations/20260801000001_admin_visibility.sql** was applied — done the same evening, founder-authorized, driven through the dashboard SQL editor in his Chrome (no psql path from this machine): all four profiles columns verified present, system_heartbeats created with relrowsecurity=true and ZERO policies (service-role only, the store_webhook_events stance). First look at the live Metrics page immediately paid for itself twice: it showed real numbers (DAU 4, share rate 5.1%% on 98 games, Pro conversion 18.52%% of 27 accounts) AND exposed a bug in the page's own bar chart — every bar rendered flat because a percentage height has no definite parent height to resolve against (fixed with h-full on the column). Then the Ops page did the same: its anti-cheat watchlist came up FULL of Gauntlet runs by BMT and Ukrainian Cyclone, flagged for total_boards hitting the clamp ceiling of 21 — except **21 is exactly a legitimate full Gauntlet** (1+4+8+4+4 across its five stages). The generic "at the clamp ceiling" heuristic was wrong for the one mode whose honest maximum IS the ceiling; the rule now applies the board check only to non-Gauntlet modes (where Octordle's 8 is the real ceiling) and drops rows left with no reason. **A watchlist that cries wolf gets ignored, which is worse than not having one — the same §197 failure mode, now in a tool built to catch failures.** **Then the founder asked for DRILL-DOWN on every number** ("see the actual data behind each"), which became one /api/admin/drill route serving the whole portal — Dashboard, Metrics, Puzzles, Ops, Payments, Referrals — returning a uniform {title, subtitle, columns, rows} so a single slide-over panel (mounted once in the admin layout) renders every metric, with per-row links to the player and CSV export. **The first click paid for the feature.** Opening "Pro conversion → 5 Pro" listed the five accounts and three had expiry dates in the PAST (Jun 30, Jul 8, Jul 31 02:30) while still counted as subscribers. Chased it properly instead of guessing: the expire-pro cron's SQL is correct, CRON_SECRET is set, the hourly schedule is registered, and the protect_pro_columns trigger explicitly passes service_role through (`auth.role()` distinct from authenticated/anon → return new) — so the tempting "the trigger is silently reverting the sweep" theory was WRONG, and checking beat asserting. What is certainly wrong is the PORTAL: every admin count filtered on `is_pro` alone while all three clients use isProActive() (flag AND expiry in the future), so the DB flag — stale by up to an hour BY DESIGN between sweeps — was inflating the founder-facing conversion rate. Fixed at all four call sites (dashboard stats, metrics summary, payments, drill), and rather than hide the gap the drill-down now labels those rows **"LAPSED — awaiting sweep"** and the card shows the drift count. **Whether the cron also has a problem is now answerable rather than arguable — the §199 heartbeats will say so at the top of the hour.** **Lesson: a number computed one way for players and another way for founders will disagree exactly when it matters, and the disagreement is invisible until something makes you look at the rows.** **Shipping a dashboard and never opening it is how §196 happened; opening it once caught two defects in the first five minutes.** Deployed web-side either way — pages degrade gracefully until the migration lands, then the numbers start flowing. **Lesson: the marginal cost of visibility is near zero when the collection already happened; the expensive part was noticing what we had.**

*

**(198) TWO DICTIONARIES, TWO WORDS OF THE DAY (2026-08-01, evening).** Founder screenshot: the home card featured SHIRE while the Past Words sheet said OTTER for the same July 31. Both surfaces run the SAME algorithm — index the 1,734-word solutions list by local day, walk forward past any candidate that is blocklisted or lacks a definition — but they consulted DIFFERENT dictionaries to decide "lacks a definition": the server checks its committed word-definitions.json (moved local months ago because dictionaryapi.dev rate-limited production), while both native cards still queried dictionaryapi.dev LIVE, word by word. Different dictionary → different skip pattern → different featured word; worse, a rate-limited lookup reads as "no definition," so two phones could disagree with EACH OTHER on a flaky network day. **Fix: the §193/§195 pattern again — single-source from the web.** Both native cards now take today's entry from /api/words (rendered by the very module that renders the archive, so card and sheet agree BY CONSTRUCTION), keeping the local walk as offline fallback only. iOS fetches with reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData (§193's lesson pre-applied). **Also shipped, at the founder's ask: /admin/words** — the upcoming featured words (next 14-120 days) with per-day skip reasons, plus the cycle mechanics he asked about: the index is daysSinceEpoch %% list length, so when it walks off the end it wraps to word #1 and the list replays in order — a full cycle every ~4.75 years, nothing breaks at the seam. The page is derived from the same module as production, so what it predicts is what ships. **Housekeeping with a lesson:** "pnpm test" mapped to bare vitest — WATCH mode — which sat forever in the non-interactive gate looking exactly like a frozen typecheck; it is now "vitest run" (watch lives at test:watch). A gate that can idle indefinitely will eventually be mistaken for a hang, and vice versa. **The §197 lesson generalizes: when two surfaces must agree, they must share the computation, not reimplement it — a faithful port of the algorithm still diverges the day its INPUTS drift.**

*

**(197) THE STREAK POPUP THAT CRIED WOLF, AND A BUILD OF PEAK-MOMENT POLISH (2026-08-01).** Founder: *"Sometimes I feel like I'll get that popup even though I have [played]."* He was right, and the bug is a multi-device one. **THE STREAK-AT-RISK CHECK JUDGED A CACHED PROFILE.** Both native apps run the shield check at launch against the profile restored from DISK — a snapshot from the last session. Play a game on web or the other phone in the morning, open the app in the evening, and the cached `last_played_at` still says yesterday: "Streak at Risk!" for a streak that is not. Web was never affected (its profile is fetched fresh at mount — the same class of stale-cache bug as §193's remote copy, wearing a different hat). **The fix refuses to judge stale data:** iOS awaits `refreshProfile()` and additionally suppresses when any daily is already completed today; Android got `ShieldService.freshStreakAtRisk(userId)` — a self-contained server read of `last_played_at` + `daily_login_streak` that returns null on failure, because *showing nothing beats a false alarm* (the old modal-caller's `LaunchedEffect(profile?.id)` latch meant the check ran exactly once, on the cached emission — the worst possible moment). **THE REST OF THE BATCH — build 142 content, all three platforms in parity:** (1) **"Streak saved!"** — using a shield used to close the modal instantly with zero feedback for spending a scarce paid-tier consumable; now the card swaps to a 🛡 *Streak saved! Your N-day streak is protected · M shields left* beat for 1.8s and dismisses itself (callers no longer close it — the modal owns its own exit on all three platforms). (2) **Rating prompt at the peak moment**: SKStoreReviewController / Play In-App Review fire 3s after a FLAWLESS Victory celebration — the single happiest moment the product has — throttled to once per 30 days on-device, with each store's own quota on top. Never on launch: an ask at a neutral moment samples neutral reviews. (3) **Widget deep links**: each cell in the medium widget is a `Link` to `wordocious://daily/<MODE>` — tap Octordle on the widget, land IN Octordle (ProperNoundle excluded: its daily is a NavigationLink with no programmatic hook, so its cell opens the app to Home rather than pretending). Custom scheme, widget-only, handled in DeepLink.swift. (4) **Lock-screen widget** (accessoryRectangular): WORDOCIOUS, dailies-played count, streak flame — with an explicitly CLEAR containerBackground, because lock-screen widgets render on the system's material, not yours. Rides with §earlier's widget streak-column fix (the widget read `current_streak`, the WIN streak that resets on any loss, while the header showed the 19-day `daily_login_streak` — same number name, different truths). **SHIPPED: Android 47 to internal for Doug; web deployed; and later the same day the founder called it — iOS 1.3 build 142 was cut (archive/export/re-sign per §9, all FOUR entitlements verified restored), uploaded, attached to the 1.3 draft, and SUBMITTED for review via the API (reviewSubmission → item → submitted:true), replacing staged-but-buildless 141 as the 1.3 binary. Same day the $0.11 Google micro-deposit landed and the founder verified the payout bank — the Android money chain (§194) is now COMPLETE end to end.** **Lesson, same as §193 and §196 wearing a third hat: a cached value is an answer to a question asked in the past. Any check that punishes the user must ask the question NOW.**

*

**(196) THE ADMIN PORTAL NEVER WORKED, AND NOBODY KNEW FOR FOUR MONTHS (2026-08-01).** Jasson: the admin page redirects me home. First instinct — he lacks the role. WRONG: the DB showed **JPGolf = admin since the beginning**, alongside BMT. The redirect was structural. **THE BUG:** middleware.ts gates /admin by reading a Supabase auth cookie — but the web client is plain supabase-js, which keeps its session in **localStorage**, and NOTHING in the codebase has ever written an auth cookie. Confirmed against production: unauthenticated or not, /admin 307'd to / for EVERYONE, founder included. Compounding it, the admin pages fetch their APIs with no Authorization header, so even past the gate every dashboard would have 401'd. **The portal shipped in April and was never once end-to-end exercised** — all real admin work happened through the SQL editor and scripts, so nothing ever surfaced it. It took a second admin trying the front door. **THE FIX (smallest blast radius, no auth-storage migration):** (1) auth-context mirrors the access token into a **wr-auth-token cookie** on every auth state change — the name contains "auth-token" so both server-side readers (middleware + verifyAdmin's cookie fallback) find it, and same-origin fetches carry it automatically, which fixed the admin APIs without touching a single call site. (2) A stale or missing token bounces to **/admin-auth**, a public relay that refreshes the session client-side, rewrites the cookie, and returns with wr_retried=1 — an admin arriving cold after an hour idle passes on the second hop instead of being dumped home; signed-out visitors exit to home; next= is clamped to /admin* against open redirects. (3) Signed-in non-admins still go straight home. **Lessons.** A gate nobody passes looks exactly like a gate that works — every bounce reads as "correctly denied." Auth plumbing needs a POSITIVE end-to-end test (an admin actually getting in), not just the negative one. And know where your session lives: localStorage and cookies are different worlds, and a server-side check can only ever see one of them. **Also today:** Jasson's requested engineering portal shipped — /portal → /admin/portal (inside this very gate, which is why the gate suddenly mattered), one source in portal-html.ts, standalone WORDOCIOUS_PORTAL.html at the repo root regenerated by scripts/gen-portal-html.mjs, drift-tested and secret-scanned. The bible is the chronicle; the portal is the map.

*

**(195) THE HELP CONTENT WAS LYING IN THREE PLACES — FOUNDER AUDIT OF EVERY MENU SURFACE (2026-08-01).** Founder read the "?" menu top to bottom and ordered a scrub. The mechanics first: everything except native Privacy/Terms is single-sourced from web files through /api/howtoplay, /api/content, /api/guides, /api/strategy and /api/words — so the entire audit shipped as ONE WEB DEPLOY, no builds (§193's caching fix is why new iOS builds see it immediately; older ones lag up to an hour). **FINDING 1 — the How to Play scoring section described the RETIRED V1 formula.** Scoring cut to guess-first V2 on 2026-07-14; the FAQ was updated then, this page was missed, and for two weeks the two surfaces CONTRADICTED each other in the same menu — V1's 120/150 hint penalties, guess bonus "only in Six, Seven and ProperNoundle", and a worked example from the dead formula. When copy exists in two places, a formula change must grep for every description of it. **FINDING 2 — "no names or places" in a game that ships ProperNoundle.** §192's Which Words Count section stated proper nouns are excluded, full stop — while an entire mode is famous names, and PN guesses skip dictionary validation entirely on all three platforms. Rewritten with the carve-out, moved below the Game Mode Guide (meet the modes before the rules about them), and the two-word-lists framing replaced by ONE "word bank" — the founder's call, and right: the answers/guesses split is implementation, not something a player should have to model. **FINDING 3 — bots existed nowhere in the copy.** VS Battle ships bot opponents with personalities and difficulty tiers; every VS description said only "get matched with a random opponent." Now: friend invite, live queue, or bot — stated in the mode guide, the help sheet and About. **Smaller kills:** PN pool "over 670" → "more than 650" (669 after curation); Gauntlet copy named 3 of its 5 stages; Support claimed Pro "unlocks extra game modes" (it does not — every mode is free); cancel instructions pointed at "profile settings" instead of the Manage Subscription row; sign-in copy omitted Apple; "refresh the page" told to app users. Guides and Strategy audited and CLEAN — they were born after the V2 cutover. **Lesson: editorial copy rots at the speed of the product, and nothing fails a build when it does. The fix that lasts is the one from §192 — the support@ line invites players to report the rot — but the periodic founder read-through is what actually caught all three.**

**(194) ANDROID CAN TAKE MONEY: THE MERCHANT ACCOUNT, THE 15%, AND WHICH PERMISSION ACTUALLY CREATES PRODUCTS (2026-08-01, morning).** The wall from §191 came down in one sitting. The founder created the **Google Payments merchant account** (business profile, legal name ShowLoud, LLC — the entity type must match the org-registered developer account, and the LEGAL name field must match IRS records exactly, while the customer-facing statement name is **Wordocious**, because a buyer scanning their card statement knows the app, not the LLC; Play renders it as GOOGLE*Wordocious). The Subscriptions page that refused with "You need to set up a Google Payments merchant account" now loads. Then the full payout chain in the same morning: **account group created → 15% service-fee enrollment accepted** (reduced rate on the first $1M/yr; the similar-brand-features question is the anti-splitting declaration — ShowLoud is the only developer account, so No) → **payout bank submitted**. Payouts sit at *Verification pending* until Google's small test deposit lands and the founder RE-ENTERS THE EXACT AMOUNT in the payments profile — it never clears on its own. That gates receiving money, not creating products or tester purchases. **THE PERMISSION TRAP:** granting the play-publisher service account rights to create the three products (pro_monthly $6.99 / pro_yearly $59.99 / pro_day $1.00 — verified against ASC, all APPROVED on Apple, IDs bare not bundle-prefixed) is NOT "Manage orders and subscriptions" — that is refunds and cancellations. It is **"Manage store presence"** (*"…edit pricing; manage in-app products…"*), which silently pulls in "Manage policy declarations" as a dependency (4→6 permissions). Read the descriptions, not the names. **"Release to production" stays OFF**, so play-upload.mjs's safety property — this key cannot ship to users — survives the widening. Two API gotchas for the eventual script (scripts/play-create-products.mjs, committed ready): a schema-invalid create returns 400 BEFORE the permission check, so a 400 is not proof of access (it fooled me — with a valid payload the same call 403s); and console permission grants take up to 24h to reach the androidpublisher API, so the post-grant 403 is lag, not error. The script prices non-US regions via Google's own converter and HALTS if that call fails rather than silently shipping US-only pricing to a 148-country app. Poller watching; products get created the moment the grant propagates.

*

**(193) THE 6/7 RUN CAUGHT A SLUR, AND REMOTE COPY THAT NEVER ARRIVED (2026-07-31).** Ran §192's rule set at 6 and 7 "just to be safe". The words were the least of it. **G5 WAS INCOMPLETE AND THE INFLECTOR WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A SLUR.** The blocklist holds NEGRO but not its plurals, and the check only looked at the DERIVED word — so both runs proposed **NEGROS and NEGROES**. Inflecting a slur produces a slur. G5 now rejects any word whose inflection BASE is blocked, keyed on the base rather than a prefix, because prefix matching condemns SQUAWK, NEGRONI and PONCEAU — ordinary words that merely start with one. The shipped 5-letter list was audited and was clean. **Generating words means generating the offensive ones too, and a blocklist written for whole words does not cover a morphology engine.** **THE 6/7 LISTS DID NOT HAVE THE 5-LETTER GAP.** They were promoted in this repo rather than inherited raw, and already carried SMOKED, ASKING, WALKED, BAGGED, STOPPED, PLANNED. Running the automatic growth anyway proposed ~350 words that were overwhelmingly surnames (ROGERS, HOLMES, JENKINS), misspellings (CATCHED, BURRIED, BEEING) and function-word plurals nobody types (AGAINS, ALONGS, ANYONES, FAMILYS). **Applying a fix where the bug is not makes things worse**, so the automatic path is switched off at those lengths. What WAS missing is real: **British spellings** — an American source lexicon meant a UK player typing HONOUR, FAVOUR, COLOURS or REALISE got "not in word list" for a word they spell correctly every day, in an app live in 148 countries. 28 added by hand. **"WILL THIS DISTURB EXISTING STATS?"** Asked before the builds, and the answer is provable rather than reassuring. Replay of a finished game — completed-daily board, VS result detail, every share card — pushes the STORED guesses back through the reducer, which silently drops any guess failing `isValidWord`. So removing a word does not just stop it being typed; **it rewrites history for everyone who played it**. Additions cannot: widening an accept-set leaves past guesses valid. The diff is a SUPERSET at every playable length — 0 removed at 5, 6 and 7; the only 54 entries dropped were never 5 letters, and all 29 of the 6/7-letter ones remain in `allowed-6/7.json` anyway. Stats themselves never touch the dictionary: `daily_results` stores materialised numbers, `scoring.ts` imports only types, and topWords aggregates stored strings (its `isRealGuessWord` is an all-letters check for hint rows, not a lookup). Pinned as three tests per length, verified non-vacuous by deleting an answer and watching them go red. **REMOTE COPY THAT NEVER ARRIVED.** Founder, on his phone, about the new How to Play section: *"I don't see it."* It was live on the web and served correctly. The phone was wrong in two stacked layers, iOS only: both content services returned early **once per launch** (`guard !loaded`), so a backgrounded app never refetched — and both used `URLSession.shared`, which **honours the response's `max-age=3600`**, so even a cold launch could serve an hour-old copy. The entire point of serving About, Support, announcements and How to Play from `/api/*` is that editing them is a DEPLOY, not a RELEASE; two layers of client staleness quietly took that property away, and nothing would have surfaced it except someone looking. Android was never affected — it installs no `HttpResponseCache`. **A cache that outlives the thing it was built to make instant is just a stale copy.** **SHIPPED:** web deployed; **Android 46** on internal (dictionary + VS fix + About); **iOS 1.3 build 141** uploaded. **1.2 (build 139) deliberately left in review untouched** — pulling it to swap in a newer binary restarts the queue for no gain, so 1.3 is staged to submit the moment 1.2 clears. Runbook correction: the export strips **FOUR** entitlements from the app, not the two documented — `aps-environment` and `associated-domains` joined `applesignin` and `application-groups` when APNs and universal links landed. All four restored before signing.

*

**(192) THE DICTIONARY HAD NO RULES — "WHY IS POSED A WORD BUT NUKED ISN'T?" (2026-07-31).** Doug asked what our logic was on `-ed` words. There was none. **POSED is accepted because it is an ANSWER** — answers are force-fed into the guess list so every shipped daily stays guessable — and it was literally that day's QuadWord answer. **NUKED is neither an answer nor a Webster's lemma, so nothing ever put it there.** The gaps were arbitrary in exactly the way that implies no rule: POKED yes / JOKED no, BAKED yes / FAKED no, LIKED yes / HIKED no, and **ASKED and BORED missing outright**. Root cause: `allowed.json` was **~91% `/usr/share/dict/words`, the 1934 Webster's, which lists only LEMMAS.** It contains no ASKED, no LOOKS, no WANTS, no BORED. Of the 205 `-ED` words we did accept, **43 were there solely because an answer forced them in** — that rule was the only reason `-ed` words worked at all. Compounding it: **every tool we had only ever CUT.** `--curate-allowed` prunes; nothing had ever added. The list sat at ~9.3k from March 2026, moving only for slur purges. It also held **54 entries that were not 5 letters** (COUPLE, HACKERS, LIAR) and could never be typed. **THE RULE SET (G1-G8, in the `curate-solutions.py` docstring):** shape; answers always guessable; lexicon-derived candidates; **G4 morphological closure** — generate `-S/-ES/-ED/-D/-ING/-ER/-IES/-IED` rather than hoping a 1934 book had them; offensive blocklist; no proper nouns; **G7 monotonic — the guess list never shrinks**, asserted in code, because a word that worked yesterday must work tomorrow; and G8, a human-vouched modern list. **G6 came free from an accident of the source:** web2 CAPITALISES proper nouns, so "appears only capitalised" identifies ~25k of them (PARIS, INDIA, DAVID, ASIAN) while CHINA and ROBIN appear both ways and survive. **Two things no lexicon could supply.** First, web2 has outright holes — **it has no entry for "box"**, which is why BOXED was missing and why a lexicon-anchored rule alone is not enough. Second, it predates EMOJI, EBOOK, CARBS. **Mining the modern corpus automatically was tried and rejected:** any threshold low enough to catch EBOOK also catches HTTPS, EVANS, NAZIS and the misspelling THIER, because words absent from web2 entirely are invisible to the capitalisation filter — there is nothing to catch them with. Hence hand-checked `modern-words.txt` and `proper-noun-blocklist.txt`. **Result: 9,330 → 10,140, zero words removed.** The safety argument that made it shippable: guess lookup is a **set membership test**, so growing the list cannot move any daily — only the ANSWER array is order-locked. Verified: **`seed-fixtures.json` came out byte-identical**, so no answer moved. One real hazard was checked and found already handled — Rescue prefills pick by `hash % pool.length`, a POSITIONAL dependency that growing a list would shift, but production passes the **answer bank**, not the guess list (a comment in `reducer.ts` records that earlier fix); only the parity *fixture* feeds in `allowed`, which is why that fixture moved and nothing else did. **How to Play gained a "Which Words Count?" section** explaining the two tiers and inviting reports to `support@wordocious.com` — no dictionary is ever finished, and players find the gaps. It is single-sourced through `/api/howtoplay`, so it reaches iOS and Android **on deploy, with no build**. **Postscript on §189:** Doug reported Succession still stalling, but the Play API showed **build 45 live on internal testing and `git merge-base` confirmed it contains the fix** — he had not taken the update. Tracing it anyway found the same bug alive in **VS**, which passed `useQuadrant` (`multiBoard && !isSequential`) where `applyToAll` belonged. The two agree everywhere except a sequential stage. `submit()` now passes the board index it had already computed, so routing is correct even when a caller gets the flag wrong — **which has now happened twice.** **Lesson: when a list has no generating rule, its contents are an accident of whatever file it was scraped from — and every later edit only ever removes.**

**(191) THE PLAY CONSOLE IS CLEAR — AND THE MERCHANT ACCOUNT IS THE WALL (2026-07-30, night).** App content went from **9 declarations needing attention to 0**. The one that mattered was invisible: **Data safety would not submit, and the blocker was the PRIVACY POLICY**, never filled in. The console surfaces that on the Preview step as a bare `error` + "Go to Privacy policy" — no banner, no named field — so it read as a broken Save button for hours. I chased the Advertising ID declaration first on the theory that declaring the ad ID as *shared* implied a dependency; wrong. The moment `https://wordocious.com/privacy` (already live, HTTP 200) was saved, Save went blue. **Lesson: when a console button is dead, scrape the page for error text before theorising about dependencies.** Also completed: Sign in details (reviewer account `bterchin+appdevtester@gmail.com`, granted Pro so the "full access" checkbox is TRUE rather than aspirational), Advertising ID (Yes / *Advertising or marketing* only — verified `com.google.android.gms.permission.AD_ID` really is in the merged release manifest before answering, and Google's own inline check agreed), Government/Financial/Health (all No), and the **IARC content rating**. **TARGET AUDIENCE — the correction that mattered:** I recommended 13+ on the reasoning that Families policy only applies under 13. Toggling it proved otherwise — **ANY under-18 selection raises the Families obligations** (certified ad networks, COPPA/GDPR, or a neutral age screen), because some territories treat under-16s as children. We serve personalised AdMob ads with a shared advertising ID and have no age screen, so 13+ would have been a declaration we don't meet. **18 and over only**, reversible later once the ad-consent work exists. Consequence worth knowing: the rating came back **Rated for 3+** (ESRB Everyone, PEGI 3, USK all ages; ClassInd 14+ is the outlier, it rates online interaction conservatively) — so Wordocious is a 3+ game restricted to adults. Play accepts the combination. **THE WALL: there is no Google Payments merchant account on this developer account.** Subscriptions and Order management both refuse with "Missing requirements", and there is no Payments profile under Settings. **This blocks all three IAP products** (`pro_monthly` $6.99 / `pro_yearly` $59.99 / `pro_day` $1.00 — identical to Apple), so Pro is unpurchasable on Android regardless of what ships. It needs the ShowLoud legal entity, tax info and a bank account: founder-only. **The three rails are independent by policy, not by choice** — Apple mandates IAP in the iOS app, Google mandates Play Billing in the Android app, Stripe is web-only. You cannot route Android purchases through Stripe. Verified against the live site rather than the runbook: `/pro` renders "Subscribe Monthly", never "Coming soon", so **Stripe is already live** and my earlier "web can't sell" claim was wrong — the runbook is a build-time checklist, not a status board. **Apple and Stripe both take money today; Android is the only rail that cannot.** Builds **44 and 45** shipped to internal testing; App Store 1.2 (build 139) is Waiting for Review with its review notes corrected — they told reviewers to tap "Play as Guest" when the button says **"Play without an account"**.

**(190) SIGN-UP WAS BROKEN ON ALL THREE PLATFORMS, IN THREE DIFFERENT WAYS (2026-07-30, night).** Founder tried creating an account by email for the first time: *"it gives zero confirmation that anything happens after you hit create account."* Email confirmation is ON, so `signUp` returns **no session** — and every platform mishandled that differently. **Web:** the success branch set no state at all, so the button looked dead. The `sent` banner already existed and only the password-reset path used it. **iOS was worse:** `signUp` ignored whether a session came back and the view called `dismiss()` regardless, closing the sheet and returning the user to a signed-out app in silence. **Android was subtler and arguably worst:** `signUpWithEmail` already returned the right words — "Check your email to confirm your account" — but as a plain `String?` alongside the real errors, and the caller rendered every non-null return in the **RED error card**. A correct sign-up looked like a failure. Fixes: iOS `signUp` now returns `Bool` (session created?); Android returns a `SignUpOutcome` sealed type where `ConfirmEmail` is explicitly a success; web sets `sent`. All three also gained a **confirm-password field and a reveal toggle** — one toggle driving both fields, so confirming means comparing what you can actually read. Without it, a typo in two masked fields silently creates an account nobody can ever sign into. **The pattern: "no session" is a SUCCESS state that reads as failure in every naive implementation, and three independent ports each got it wrong in a different direction.**

**(189) THE GAUNTLET STALL — A MODE TEST WHERE A STAGE TEST BELONGED — AND HOW TO SCOPE AN AUDIT (2026-07-30, night).** Doug: Gauntlet played fine until the **Succession** stage, then the word would not advance — the same freeze the standalone mode had before it was fixed. That was the clue. The standalone fix derived the active board as *the first still-PLAYING one* instead of the never-advancing `currentBoardIndex`, but gated it on **`mode == SEQUENCE`**. Inside Gauntlet the mode is `GAUNTLET` — **it is the STAGE that is sequential** — so `activeBoardIndex` fell through to `currentBoardIndex` (0) forever. Meanwhile the screen's own copy of the predicate *did* have the stage clause and reported `isSequential = true`, so board 0 was won and therefore not active, boards 1..n were locked because `boardIdx > 0`, and **nothing accepted input**. The predicate existed in **four places and two omitted the Gauntlet clause**; collapsed to one `isSequentialStage` on the ViewModel. `VSGameScreen` carried the same mode-only test, so VS Gauntlet's Succession stage would have rendered as QuadWord with a frozen board. iOS (`isSequence`) and web (`currentStageConfig.sequential`) had keyed off the stage all along. **THE BIGGER LESSON WAS ABOUT AUDITING.** Asked to sweep Gauntlet, ProperNoundle, VS and Sweep for render parity, I scoped each by **filename** — "VS" became `ui/vs/VS*.kt`, which silently excluded `data/BotEngine.kt`, `BotMatchService.kt` and `BotPersonas.kt`, i.e. **the opponent most VS matches actually use**. "Sweep" became `SweepCelebration.kt` and stopped there. The founder caught four surfaces in a row this way — bots, the spectate/skip screen, the results share button, and the share-card variants — each time asking "did you check X?" and the answer being no. **Grepping filenames finds files; it does not find flows.** Enumerate the screens a player passes through, then find the code behind each. Real defects that only surfaced once the flows were audited: the **share card painted `HINT_USED` amber** where iOS folds it in with `absent` (grey), so the same game shared from each phone produced visibly different cards; **ProperNoundle's post-game board dropped `wordGroups`**, so "Taylor Swift" played as 6+5 tiles and then finished as eleven undifferentiated squares; the **spectate "Skip to result" CTA** was a text-hugging chip where iOS makes it the full-width primary action; and the Gauntlet results screen used a **second, divergent stage list** that lost the per-stage times and opened a **hardcoded-white dialog** — unreadable in dark mode — instead of expanding in place. One judgment call, founder-confirmed: iOS uses the **broom as the sweep card's header icon** and a **trophy in the empty state**; Android drew the broom in both, so the trophy stays.

**(188) THE ANSWER BANK WAS SIZED, NOT CURATED — AND FOUR OTHER TESTER FINDS (2026-07-30, evening).** The tester queried the Deliverance clue rows: **PASSO, LYTTA, HOGGY**. He was right — LYTTA is cartilage in a dog's tongue. Prefills were drawn from `getAllowedWords()`, the ~13k list of everything LEGAL TO TYPE, instead of the curated answer bank. **Same mistake as the bot filler pool (task #3): the guess list is a tempting "big list of words" and it is the wrong one nearly every time.** Fixed at all three call sites on all three engines; 2026-07-30 now clues PURGE / PRONE / STIPE. Parity fixtures were untouched because they exercise `generatePrefillWords()` directly — the CALLERS changed, not the function. **THEN THE REAL QUESTION: why does STIPE keep happening?** Founder: "we've done this exercise a few times and I always end up thinking we are in good shape." Three curation passes, three surprises — so the METHOD was wrong, not the effort. `curate-solutions.py` warned when the kept count left [2100,3000] and said "adjust --threshold", so every pass walked the bar DOWN until enough words survived. The fingerprint is unmistakable: **every rarest word in the bank sat at exactly the then-threshold** — GUNNY, PAISA, SOPPY, BARMY all at zipf 2.30. That is not a curated floor, that is a headcount. **INVERTED: threshold is the INPUT, count is the OUTPUT.** The size anxiety was misplaced — 2.3 bought 2,412 words (6.6 yrs); 3.0 buys 1,818 (5.0 yrs). We were paying 25% more bank for years nobody will play, and buying exactly the words that draw complaints. **FREQUENCY ALONE IS NOT ENOUGH — the part that would have caused a fourth failure.** PASHA (3.14) and FOOTY (3.15) CLEAR a 3.0 floor and are still wrong: wordfreq blends British English, historical text, brands and clinical writing, so no threshold separates "statistically common" from "in a player's head". Hence a second, human gate over the 3.0–3.5 band only — 582 words, triaged by me to 86 grouped by REASON (brands, clinical/bodily, British, archaic, loanwords, obscure). Founder ruled: cut 85, **keep BANJO** — correctly; it is an instrument, not a brand, and my grouping was wrong. Final bank **2412 → 1734** (-678 frequency, -85 review). The 85 live in `manual-blocklist.txt`, NOT edited into the JSON, so they survive every future re-curation — otherwise the next run silently readmits them, which is how the list rotted in the first place. **A CORRECTION WORTH KEEPING:** I claimed the bank could not be regenerated without building a multi-era cutover mechanism. Founder pushed back — "we've done the cutover smoothly before, I don't think we built anything like you're describing" — and the git log proved him right: the bank has been regenerated FOUR times, three of them AFTER the 2026-07-08 cutover (`bfa56a5`, `4cb5576`, `1395462`), because `solutions-legacy.json` is frozen and only ever read. **Advancing the cutover would have been actively worse** — it would send July's dailies back to the UNCURATED legacy list. Lesson: check the log before asserting an architectural constraint. **ALSO THIS EVENING:** Deliverance/answer work aside — Play **Data safety** filled end to end (every answer traced to code in `docs/play-data-safety.md`; the advertising ID is SHARED because AdMob receives it, and the app DOES carry user-generated content); Play rejected `/settings` as the account-deletion URL with a **404** — settings is a modal, not a route — so `/delete-account` now exists, and writing it surfaced that **deleting an account does not cancel Pro** (the store holds it); the **username policy** moved into a DB trigger (an app-side check is one curl from irrelevant) with the Scunthorpe problem handled by STRIPPING known-innocent words before the scan; **ad-consent withdrawal** added on both natives (UMP requires a persistent entry point); web **multi-board a11y** (the feedback is entirely colour — a screen reader got five bare letters); iOS **Dynamic Type** finally does something (there was no UIFontMetrics anywhere, capped at 1.6x because a puzzle board is geometry, not flow); and the **API 36 rendered sweep** — light and dark — which caught two system-bar defects invisible to source review, one of them behind a comment that asserted the opposite of what the code did. **(187) GIFTING PRO WAS FREE, AND ANDROID SIGN-IN GETS A DOOR THAT ISN'T THE DEVICE (2026-07-30).** Doug — a brand-new FREE account, 0 shields, no subscription — was shown "GIFT PRO TO FRIENDS" on his first visit to the profile tab, and the same panel was live on iOS 1.1. **Nothing anywhere checked whether the person gifting Pro had Pro.** The panel was built under the referral plan's open question "Who can invite", which assumed any signed-in player; that default turned a paid perk into a free one. The real exposure was not the button: `/api/referrals/create` is a SERVICE-ROLE endpoint that writes Pro entitlement, and it never resolved the caller's own profile — so any session could POST it directly and mint 7-day Pro for three people at a time, recycling slots forever. FIX: the endpoint now requires `isProActive` on the caller (`maybeSingle` + `isProActive` fails closed — a missing row or lapsed expiry is not Pro), and all three client panels hide for non-Pro. **The gate closed the hole on the LIVE App Store build the moment web deployed**, with no submission: every client — web, iOS 1.1, Android — creates invites through that one endpoint, and `referrals` RLS grants SELECT only with no client INSERT policy, so there is no second door. A free user on the shipped binary still SEES the panel but gets the 403 copy. DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED: invites already created by free accounts still redeem — their friends were promised 7 days, and revoking a sent gift is worse than the bug. iOS 1.2 build 139 cut, VALID, attached and submitted (WAITING_FOR_REVIEW). **The Android half: Google one-touch was NOT fixable on its own.** "Account reauth failed" means the DEVICE's Google account cannot mint an ID token; the bottom sheet and the account picker BOTH go through Credential Manager, so the two-shot retry from §186 could never help, and the previous build's only answer was an error telling a tester to go fix it in system Settings. Our OAuth client, audience and SHA-1s were verified clean. The browser flow doesn't touch the device account: Supabase opens Google's consent page in a Custom Tab, the user authenticates with their BROWSER Google session, and Google redirects to `wordocious://auth-callback`. Same provider, same Supabase user, same profiles row — only the token minter differs. Now used as the fallback on reauth failure AND on NoCredentialException (no usable account, or a signing SHA-1 still propagating). The return leg needs its own profile load + authenticated flip, because the browser path never comes back through `signInWithGoogle`. **Requires a console change to work: `wordocious://auth-callback` must be added to Supabase → Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs.** versionCode 36 on internal testing. **LESSON — a workaround offered as a fix is a wrong answer.** When one-touch failed I told the founder to have Doug use Forgot password. He pushed back: "I don't understand why doug would do forgot password when we are trying to get the google one touch working." He was right. Unblocking a tester and fixing the feature are different jobs, and presenting the first as the second buries the second. **HELD BACK ON PURPOSE:** two agents finished ~720 lines across the payments and offline-retry paths, all three platforms compiling. The payments half moves the +4 streak shields entirely to the store webhooks — correct in principle (the client half was forgeable AND double-granting, so subscribers got 8/period; it also read-modify-wrote `streak_shields` from a null launch profile, zeroing a paying subscriber's balance on a Day Pass) — but **it could not be confirmed that the Apple ASSN webhook is actually receiving anything**: no service-role key locally to query `store_webhook_events`, and the runbook does not record the notification URL as configured in ASC. If ASSN is not live, that change silently ends shield grants for every subscriber. The Android half of it rode build 36 (Play billing isn't live, so it's inert there); the iOS half was STASHED out of build 139 rather than discovered in review. VERIFY ASSN BEFORE SHIPPING THE iOS HALF. **(184) ANDROID↔iOS PARITY: THE FULL SWEEP (2026-07-30).** Founder asked for an "all encompassing" pre-sim audit — Android has been built alongside iOS with parity done live, and he wanted to know the real gap before looking at a simulator. 15 surfaces audited by one agent each, every finding then re-checked by an ADVERSARIAL verifier told to refute and to default to REFUTED when unsure (it was primed against the classic cross-platform false positives: SF Symbol point size vs Compose Icon box size, Capsule vs CircleShape on a wide box, .weight vs fixed frames). 273 raw → 258 CONFIRMED → 236 fixed. Split: 52 high / 123 medium / 83 low; 121 behavioral, 137 visual+copy. THE FOUNDATION WAS FINE — all four palettes match iOS hex-for-hex, Nunito is on every text role, no screen is missing, 104 Swift files vs 105 Kotlin. Every defect was in widgets built ON TOP, and clustered on one pattern: Android reached for a MATERIAL DEFAULT where iOS draws something specific (NavigationBar's tonal pill vs iOS's 4dp dot; HelpOutline — itself a circled ? — inside our circular chrome; LocalFireDepartment's solid flame beside our stroked outline shield). WORST FINDS: (a) MaterialTheme.colorScheme was a top-level val, so it froze at launch AND was always lightColorScheme — theme switches left Material chrome on the old palette until restart and Dark got light dialogs even after one; (b) three money bugs in StoreManager — Day Pass consumed before the grant was confirmed, a failed entitlement write still marking the order consumed (that period's +4 shields lost forever, no retry), and launch reconcile able to zero streak_shields by racing profile load; (c) PRO badge keyed off raw is_pro not isProActive, so EXPIRED subscribers kept the gold badge and never saw the upsell; (d) the bot solved Succession/Gauntlet boards in ONE guess; (e) the forfeit dialog claimed "counts as a loss" during CPU practice, which the code explicitly does not record; (f) PresenceService was fully implemented and called from NOWHERE, so Android players never appeared in the LIVE count. **PROCESS FAILURE WORTH REMEMBERING: subagents inherit the session cwd.** Running inside a git worktree, some fix agents resolved paths against the main checkout and others against the worktree — which sat on af8fa6b and lacked the round-1/2 parity commits. An absolute repo path in the PROMPT is not enough. The first "BUILD SUCCESSFUL" proved only half the tree. Recovered by committing BOTH halves verbatim before merging (30dcab1 + 8ecee88 → d7afd17), 4 duplicate-fix conflicts resolved on merit, markers from both halves verified present after. NOT DONE: Android push (no Firebase project / google-services.json — task #72, needs Brian's Google account, pairs with #49) and real AdMob unit ids (no Android AdMob app exists; Google TEST ids still shipping). Also decided AGAINST matching iOS on the Settings "Done" button: iOS renders it #007AFF only because SwiftUI toolbar buttons default to system blue — a framework default, not a design decision, and system blue reads as foreign chrome on Android. Board tiles went the other way: iOS rounds (size*0.14) while web AND Android were square, so per the brief Android was rounded to match iOS and the WEB was rounded too (rounded-[14%], a percentage radius so it tracks the clamp() sizing). Gates: compileDebugKotlin clean, Android unit tests green, tsc clean, vitest 48/48. All Android work is SOURCE-ONLY and rides the first Play build (#49/#50 still the blocker). **(§183) GAUNTLET STAGE INTERSTITIAL — discoverable skip + VS no longer costs you the race (2026-07-29).** Founder hit this in a live VS Gauntlet against a bot: the STAGE COMPLETE / NEXT UP overlay felt frozen, and when it cleared the bot had played ahead. Investigation found the overlay was ALREADY tap-to-skip on all three platforms (web `onClick`, iOS `.onTapGesture`, Android `clickableNoRipple`) — no code path blocks the tap, and the freeze could not be reproduced from the source. The REAL defects were the two underneath: **(1) nothing advertised the tap except the final stage** (iOS showed a 'Tap to see your results' pill only when `next == nil`), so mid-run it read as an unskippable cutscene — every platform now shows a 'Tap to continue' line under the next-stage detail; **(2) the 2.5s interstitial does not pause the OPPONENT'S CLOCK**, so in VS it was a handicap scaling with how many stages you cleared (~12s across a 5-stage run) — VS now uses 1s (solo keeps 2.5s; the post-final-stage pause stays 4s on both, since the win only records on advance — see the Flawless-as-8/9-Sweep note in the code). Implemented via an `isVersus` flag threaded from the VS call sites on all three platforms. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48, iOS + Android BUILD SUCCEEDED. Queued for the next native build. **(§182) VS MATCH HIJACK — a public user id was enough to steal a live match (2026-07-29).** Asking 'is the VS server set up the best way?' surfaced the worst bug of the audit. The socket handshake took `presenceId` ("u:<userId>") as a PLAIN STRING and trusted it. User ids are public (records/leaderboard render /profile/<uuid> links). **DEMONSTRATED against a local server:** victim in a match disconnects → 60s reconnect grace opens → attacker connects with the victim's presenceId and no token/password → `match_resumed`, attacker owns the match. **FIX (apps/server/src/auth.ts):** the project signs tokens ES256 and publishes a JWKS, so the server verifies signatures locally — no shared secret, no API key, no per-connection round trip (keys cached 1h, refetched once on an unknown kid). `dsaEncoding: 'ieee-p1363'` again mandatory (JWS is raw r||s, not DER). **TRANSITION DESIGN (matters):** shipped clients send no token, so requiring one would break VS for everyone mid-match. Instead: valid token → verified, and presenceId is DERIVED from the token so it can't be forged; invalid token → connection rejected outright (a bad token is an attack, never a legacy client); no token → legacy, allowed but flagged, AND `pendingReconnects` now records whether the dropped socket was verified — a verified player's match can never be resumed by an unverified socket. The hole closes per-user as clients update; drop the legacy branch once adoption is complete. Verified by test: forged token rejected, legacy match still works, legacy resume unchanged, harness 32/32. **ARCHITECTURE VERDICT:** the VS server being separate from Vercel is FORCED, not a choice — serverless can't hold WebSockets. It runs on Railway behind the stable `server.wordocious.com` CNAME (good: the host can change without touching clients); `NEXT_PUBLIC_SERVER_URL` / VSConfig.swift / VSConfig.kt all point there. Deleted **render.yaml** — vestigial config for a host nothing uses, which briefly misled this audit. Added **graceful shutdown**: SIGTERM/SIGINT now emit `opponent_left` to every live match before closing, so a deploy no longer leaves both players staring at a board whose opponent will never move. **KNOWN, DELIBERATELY DEFERRED:** match state is in-memory Maps — a restart still ENDS live matches (now honestly) and the server cannot scale past one instance. Redis-backed state is the fix; not worth the infra, cost and bug surface at this scale. Revisit when concurrent matches make a restart actually expensive. **(§181) streak_shields WAS A SELF-GRANTABLE PAID BENEFIT — grants moved server-side (2026-07-29).** `protect_pro_columns` pins exactly seven columns (gold/silver/bronze_medals, is_admin, is_banned, is_pro, pro_expires_at) and its bypass idiom is `if auth.role() is distinct from 'authenticated' and is distinct from 'anon' then return new` (service_role writes freely) plus `if not coalesce(old.is_admin,false)` (admins exempt). **streak_shields was never on that list** — yet it is +4 per Pro billing period and the referral milestone reward. Any signed-in user could PATCH profiles and mint a paid benefit. **THE TRAP THAT NEARLY BROKE PAYING CUSTOMERS:** the App Store webhook carried an explicit comment saying it does NOT grant shields because the mobile CLIENT grants the +4 per period. So simply pinning the column would have silently stopped every mobile subscriber's shields. Checked before writing the guard. Fixed properly instead: appstore AND playstore webhooks now grant RENEWAL_SHIELDS=4 server-side (Day Pass excluded; the existing eventId/store_webhook_events idempotency covers retries), matching what stripe-fulfillment already did. New **/api/shields/grant-milestone** owns the 7-day grant — it recomputes the streak from daily_results (NOT from the client-written daily_login_streak, which would just move the forgery one step back) and is idempotent via new column **profiles.last_shield_milestone** (applied + backfilled to floor(daily_login_streak/7): 1 of 22 profiles credited, so nobody is re-paid). All three clients now call it; the natives had the shield bundled INTO the same progression UPDATE as xp/streaks, so it was unbundled. **DEFERRED (task #67/#68):** manual-migrations/20260729000007 carries two commented-out triggers behind a release gate — `protect_shield_grants` (clients may SPEND shields, never mint) and `guard_progression_monotonic` (xp/wins may only rise; daily streak +1 or reset; best_* may only rise and never exceed the live value). Progression columns stay client-written by design on all 3 platforms, so monotonicity is the enforceable guard rather than pinning; it makes 'set it to 9999' impossible, which is the practical exploit against the Records rankings. Apply once a build with the server-side grant is live and adopted. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48, iOS + Android BUILD SUCCEEDED. **(§180) SWEEP LEADERBOARD WAS FORGEABLE — award moved server-side (2026-07-29).** RLS/trigger sweep of prod found `daily_bonuses` client-writable (`auth.uid() = user_id` on INSERT/UPDATE) with NO validating trigger — and `alltime_sweep_leaderboard` ranks players by COUNTING rows there with `sweep_awarded = true`. Any signed-in user could POST bonus rows for arbitrary past dates and own the all-time sweep board. (Bounded to one row per day by the unique index daily_bonuses_user_day_idx — which lives in pg_indexes, NOT pg_constraint, so an earlier constraint-only query wrongly suggested unlimited duplicates. Check both.) **THE VALIDATION THAT SAVED THE FIX:** the obvious trigger — 'sweep_awarded requires 9 completed modes' — was run against all 54 real bonus rows FIRST. It would have REJECTED 16 legitimate sweeps (2026-04-21 → 2026-05-20): the daily mode set changed on 2026-05-21 when Six/Seven (DUEL_6/DUEL_7) took it from 7 modes to 9. Hard-coding today's count would have encoded a rule that was never universally true and would break again at the next mode change. **Option 1 (server owns the grant) shipped instead**, mirroring the Pro-entitlement stance: new /api/daily/award-bonuses verifies the Bearer token, recomputes the award from daily_results with the service role, and derives the REQUIRED mode count from that day's own results rather than a constant. All three clients now call it (web lib/daily-service, iOS MedalService, Android MedalService) instead of writing the row. **THE RLS LOCK IS DELIBERATELY NOT APPLIED YET** — supabase/manual-migrations/20260729000006_DEFERRED_lock_daily_bonuses.sql carries a release gate: iOS 133 is LIVE and 136 is on TestFlight, both writing this table directly, so dropping the policy today would silently kill sweep/flawless bonuses for every existing player. Apply only once a build with the server-side award is live and adopted. Until then the hole stays open — breaking real players' sweeps is worse than a leaderboard nobody has attacked. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48, iOS + Android BUILD SUCCEEDED. **(§179) VS CRASH/FAKE-WIN, RPC CLAMP, PAYMENTS CLEAN — plus a commit that lied (2026-07-29).** **(a) THE COMMIT THAT LIED.** §177's pre-start guard was NEVER COMMITTED: it was edited in the DEV-FOLDER checkout while the commit was taken from the WORKTREE, so d8f0593 shipped the harness describing a fix it didn't contain, and I reported it as done. Lesson: with a worktree in play, edit and commit in the SAME tree, and verify with `git show HEAD:<path> | grep` rather than trusting the diff you remember writing. Both guards are now in beac93f. **(b) VS CRASH — one packet kills every match.** `submit_guess` read `match.solutions[boardIndex]` unchecked; an out-of-range index made it undefined and the `.toUpperCase()` below threw INSIDE the socket handler, taking the process down and every live match with it. **(c) VS FAKE-WIN.** `board_solved` did `boardsSolved++` with no range check and no dedup — and multi-board modes derive the winner from that count, so spamming the event let a client declare victory. Now counts DISTINCT valid indices only (`boardsSolved = solvedSet.size`). Both covered by harness scenario 9 (24/24 checks pass). **(d) SWEEP RPC CLAMP.** daily_sweep_leaderboard / alltime_sweep_leaderboard passed p_limit straight into LIMIT and are granted to **anon**, so an unauthenticated caller could demand p_limit=100000000 repeatedly. Clamped to `least(greatest(coalesce(p_limit,50),1),100)`; applied to prod (manual-migrations/20260729000005). NOTE ON METHOD: I first hand-rewrote those function bodies from memory and nearly shipped it — the real ones use `p_day::date`, an explicit 9-mode list INCLUDING DUEL_6/DUEL_7, and `count(distinct game_mode) = 9`. The migration is now generated by extracting the bodies VERBATIM from the original file and patching only the LIMIT line. Never retype a function you're only trying to clamp. **(e) PAYMENTS: CLEAN, no changes.** Stripe verifies via constructEvent + webhook secret and dedupes through the store_webhook_events ledger; Apple verifies real JWS via SignedDataVerifier and dedupes on notificationUUID; Play authenticates via a pubsub token + verifies against the Play Developer API. Critically, both mobile paths write an ABSOLUTE expiry from the store (`Math.max(exp, storedExpiryMs)`, `expiryTimeMillis`) rather than adding days, so a retried delivery cannot double-grant. The referral hook added earlier today sits behind reward_granted_at and doesn't change that. **(§178) THREE IDORs — API routes that trusted a body-supplied userId (2026-07-29).** Swept every route under apps/web/app/api for an auth gate. Found three that identified the user from the REQUEST BODY with no token check at all. This matters because **user UUIDs are public**: the records and leaderboard pages render `/profile/<uuid>` links, so ids are harvestable from page source by anyone. **(1) /api/stripe/portal — the bad one.** POST `{userId}` returned a Stripe Customer Portal session for that user: their billing history, their card details, and a working CANCEL SUBSCRIPTION button. Harvest an id from the leaderboard, cancel a paying customer's subscription. **(2) /api/push/subscribe** — upserted push_subscriptions `onConflict: user_id`, so anyone could repoint another player's notifications at their own endpoint (read their nudges) or just overwrite it to silence them. **(3) /api/push/unsubscribe** — deleted any user's subscription by id. Also hardened **/api/purchase** (lower severity — the checkout session's metadata names the grantee, so a spoofed id meant PAYING to give someone else Pro, griefing rather than theft). All four now resolve the user via `verifyUser(req)` from the Bearer token and IGNORE any id in the body; callers (settings-dialog, pro page, notification-toggle, lib/push-notifications) updated to send `session.access_token`. Verified clean afterwards: the only remaining body-supplied userIds are inside CRON_SECRET-gated backfills and the verifyAdmin-gated admin routes, where they're a deliberate scope parameter. The 4 admin backfill routes flagged by the sweep are NOT holes — they authenticate with CRON_SECRET rather than verifyAdmin. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48. RULE going forward: an API route must never take an actor id from the request body. **(§177) VS IS NOW TESTABLE WITHOUT A SECOND HUMAN — headless harness + a real exploit fixed (2026-07-29).** VS was the least-tested surface for a structural reason: every scenario needs two people on two devices simultaneously. Key discovery reading apps/server/src/index.ts: the socket handshake takes `presenceId` (`u:<userId>`) as a PLAIN STRING with no token verification, so the protocol is fully scriptable. **New apps/server/test/vs-harness.mjs** (`npm run test:vs` in apps/server, socket.io-client added as a devDependency) drives two real socket clients through 8 scenarios / 20 checks in seconds: matchmaking pairing + identical seed, pre-start guessing, invalid words, abandon, disconnect grace, double-queue, third-party match spoofing, and self-reported wins. Run against a LOCAL server only (`npx tsx src/index.ts`) — matches are real. **THE BUG IT FOUND: `submit_guess` had no `serverStartAt` check.** Guesses submitted during the 4-second countdown were fully scored (`isValid:true`). Since endMatch times players as `completedAt - match.serverStartAt`, a modified client could bank guesses pre-start and finish with a NEGATIVE duration — sorting ahead of every honest result on time-based tiebreaks. Fixed: reject with `isValid:false, reason:'Match has not started'` before the dictionary check; real clients don't accept input until match_start, so only abuse is affected. **TWO HARNESS SELF-OWNS worth remembering** (both are the same class of error — treating absence as proof): (1) the first run reported the two SPOOFING scenarios as PASSING while the server was actually dead — I'd killed it by piping its stdout to `head`. Negative assertions ('outsider could NOT do X') are satisfied trivially by a dead socket. Fixed with `requireLive()` guards on every negative check. (2) A working forfeit was reported as broken because `opponent_left` is emitted with NO PAYLOAD, and the waiter resolved `undefined`, which the `!!result` check read as 'never arrived'. Fixed by resolving payload-less events to `{}`. Everything else audited CLEAN: third parties cannot act in a match they're not in, `player_completed` is not blindly trusted, disconnect grace works, the queue never self-matches. Final: 20/20 checks PASS. **(§176) THE TWO ACCEPTED RISKS, CLOSED (2026-07-29).** §175 knowingly left two things; both are now fixed properly. **(a) Milestone-shield race → atomic RPC.** The inviter payout was four round trips (count → extend Pro → read shields → write shields+4), so simultaneous redemptions could double-grant or lose an update. New `grant_referral_inviter_rewards(p_inviter, p_instant_days, p_cap, p_milestone_at, p_milestone_shields)` — SECURITY DEFINER, service_role-only EXECUTE, applied to prod 2026-07-29 (supabase/manual-migrations/20260729000004) — does the whole decision in ONE transaction, serialized per inviter by `select ... for update` on their profile row (different inviters never block each other). Pro extension uses `greatest(coalesce(pro_expires_at, now()), now()) + make_interval(...)`, the SQL twin of the Math.max additive pattern. The milestone is now IDEMPOTENT rather than count-equality: new column `profiles.referral_milestone_at` is stamped once and checked thereafter, so `count == 3` can no longer fire twice (race) or never (if a redemption is revoked and the count re-crosses 3). **(b) Save-ownership upgrade hole → discardUnattributedSaves.** A device sitting SIGNED OUT across the update had no owner recorded, so the next account inherited the prior player's boards. Closed by the observation that makes it free: the OLD build already wiped saves on sign-out, so a signed-out device has no saves worth keeping — any files still there are unattributable. Both natives now discard them once on a signed-out launch when no owner has ever been recorded, then the normal claim takes over. Nothing real is destroyed and the leak is gone. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48, iOS + Android BUILD SUCCEEDED. **(§175) HARDENING AUDIT — 5 areas, 4 real bugs found & fixed (2026-07-29).** Post-136 hunt across the highest-risk surfaces. **(1) REFERRALS — the money bug.** `redeemReferral` claimed the row with `.update().eq('id').eq('status','pending')` and only checked for an ERROR — but PostgREST reports an update matching ZERO rows as success. Two simultaneous claims of the same code both saw 'no error' and both paid out: double 7-day trial to the friend, double +3d to the inviter. The invitee_id unique index doesn't cover this (it fires when one account claims two DIFFERENT codes, not when two requests race for one). Fixed by adding `.select('id')` and treating ZERO RETURNED ROWS as 'already_redeemed' — the returned rows, not the absence of an error, are what say you won a compare-and-swap. Inviter payout also wrapped in try/catch: the row is claimed and the friend's trial granted by that point, so a failure paying the INVITER must not report failure to the FRIEND (who'd retry and be told the code was used). Known/accepted: milestone-shield count is racy (cosmetic, needs an atomic RPC). **(2) ACCOUNT DELETION — data-loss ordering.** Deleted 7 data tables FIRST and the auth user LAST, with no error checking between. A failure on that last call left a working login with every stat, result, medal and purchase destroyed — unrecoverable. Since `profiles.id references auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` and every user table cascades from profiles, deleting the AUTH USER alone removes everything: reordered so the destructive step is atomic (all or nothing, safely retryable), with a logged post-cascade sweep as belt-and-braces. **(3) DATE/ROLLOVER — clean.** toLocalDayString/getYesterdayLocal handle month, year and DST boundaries correctly; streak logic (same-day no-op / yesterday +1 / else reset to 1) is sound. Noted: the new lapsed-push cutoff compares a UTC-derived date against LOCAL day strings — off-by-one for extreme timezones, absorbed by the 2-day window, left as-is. **(4) PRO EXPIRY — clean.** The expire-pro sweep never touches a future/null expiry; isProActive is expiry-aware on all 3 platforms. **(5) SELF-AUDIT of the same-day fixes — found a leak in my own.** The new iOS entitlement cache is keyed to NOBODY and survived sign-out, so the next account to sign in rendered as Pro (badge on, ads hidden) for the second before their own profile loaded — someone else's subscription briefly wearing their name. Now cleared in signOut(). Also flagged (accepted): the save-ownership fix has a narrow upgrade-path hole — a device sitting SIGNED OUT across the update has no recorded owner, so the next account inherits the prior player's boards; closing it would mean wiping everyone's boards once on update, which is the bug being fixed. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48, iOS BUILD SUCCEEDED. **(§174) EU 27 LIVE + seller name confirmed on every storefront (2026-07-29).** The DSA trader declaration (§160) finished propagating: Wordocious is now purchasable on the EU storefronts — verified live via the iTunes lookup API on **DE, FR, IT and ES**, all reporting seller **Showloud, LLC** and version 1.0. Combined with the US storefront, the entity flip from 'BRIAN MAXWELL TERCHIN' to the LLC is complete everywhere it's been checked, so the §159/§160 monitors are closed. Note for future launches: DSA propagation ran ~1 day behind the entity going active, and the App Store SEARCH index lagged the listing itself — the app was directly linkable and API-visible while an exact-name search for 'Wordocious' still returned nothing on-device (new-app indexing takes 24-72h and ranks by engagement, so a zero-download app sits below established word games even on its own name). Don't debug a listing that's already resolvable by lookup. **(§173) APNs SEND PATH BUILT — push can now actually fire (2026-07-29).** §171 shipped token CAPTURE; this closes the loop with delivery. **The key already existed** — 'ShowLoud APNs' (key ID **YMU68Y2US3**, created 2026/05/05, Team Scoped/All topics, Sandbox & Production) with the .p8 still in ~/Downloads; copied to ~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_YMU68Y2US3.p8 (chmod 600). Apple allows exactly ONE .p8 download ever — losing it means revoke + recreate, so treat that file as irreplaceable. New **apps/web/lib/push/apns.ts**: token-based provider auth (no cert rotation), ES256 JWT signed with node:crypto, HTTP/2 via node:http2 (Apple requires h2 — fetch won't do), one session per batch at 20-way concurrency, provider token memoized 50min (Apple: valid 1h, refresh no faster than 20min). **GOTCHA that costs a day if missed: `dsaEncoding: 'ieee-p1363'` is mandatory** — Node signs ECDSA as DER by default and JWS needs raw r||s; without it every push returns 403 InvalidProviderToken. Covered by a test that verifies the signature with a generated P-256 key. `classifyApnsFailure` decides deletions: ONLY 410 / BadDeviceToken / DeviceTokenNotForTopic are 'stale' (row deleted); 429 + 5xx are 'transient' and must NEVER purge tokens or one Apple outage silently unsubscribes everyone. sendApns never throws — push is best-effort and can't fail its caller. Wired into the existing **/api/cron/daily-reminder** (14:00 UTC): web push and APNs are parallel channels for the same nudge, users who already played are skipped, and anyone reached by web push is deduped out of the APNs pass so a PWA+app user isn't pinged twice; device_tokens is pruned here (the only place Apple reports dead tokens). Both push routes now pin `runtime = 'nodejs'` (node:http2 is absent on edge). Also **/api/admin/push/test** + a 'Send test push to my devices' button on the admin Content tab — self-targeted BY DESIGN (no way to aim it at another user; the cron is the only path that notifies anyone else). Gates: tsc clean, vitest 48/48 (39 + 9 new APNs tests). REMAINING (Brian, one step): paste the .p8 contents into Vercel as **APNS_PRIVATE_KEY** plus APNS_KEY_ID=YMU68Y2US3, APNS_TEAM_ID=Q32F6GRDYG (APNS_BUNDLE_ID defaults to com.wordocious.app); until then the route reports configured:false and sends nothing. Android/FCM still deferred to the Play build. **(§172) SIGNING TRAP — entitlements silently stripped from builds 134/135; ship.sh now gates on it (2026-07-29).** Cutting v1.1 for the native-parity batch exposed a pipeline bug that had been latent since the entitlements file grew. Builds 134 AND 135 uploaded, processed, and were marked **VALID by Apple** — with `com.apple.developer.associated-domains` and `aps-environment` MISSING from the signed binary. The app would have installed and run fine while universal links (password reset, email confirm, VS invites) silently opened Safari and push tokens could never register — i.e. the two headline features of the release, dead, with zero signal from Apple. TWO causes, one masking the other: (1) the App ID never had PUSH_NOTIFICATIONS enabled, and the 'Wordocious AppStore w/ groups' distribution profile had gone **INVALID** — adding the Associated Domains capability (§170) invalidates every existing profile. Fixed via the ASC API (scratchpad asc_fix.rb pattern): POST /v1/bundleIdCapabilities PUSH_NOTIFICATIONS, DELETE the dead profile, POST a fresh IOS_APP_STORE profile bound to the distribution cert, then write the base64 profileContent into ~/Library/MobileDevice/Provisioning Profiles/. (2) THE REAL ONE: ship.sh archives with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`, so export does NOT carry Wordocious.entitlements through — it derives entitlements from the profile, and the RESIGN step then rebuilds app_ent.plist from `codesign -d` output. That step only ever explicitly re-added applesignin + application-groups, which is why those two always survived and anything NEW vanished. **Rule: every entitlement the app needs must be re-stated in ship.sh's resign block — the .entitlements file alone does nothing for App Store builds.** Fixed by adding associated-domains + aps-environment there, plus a new `== ENTITLEMENT CHECK ==` stage that greps the SIGNED binary for all four required keys and **aborts before upload** if any is missing (Apple's VALID is not verification — check the binary, not the intent). Build 136 is the first clean v1.1: all four entitlements verified present post-sign. 134/135 are dead weight in TestFlight — do not test on them, the features you'd check are exactly the broken ones. **(§171) NATIVE PARITY BATCH — seams 1-3 + adjacent gaps + Sweep yesterday dropdown (2026-07-29).** The three genuine native seams are closed. (1) Password reset now completes IN-APP on both natives: universal/app links claim /auth/reset and /auth/confirm (AASA components + Android manifest pathPrefixes added), the link's one-time code is exchanged in-app (iOS `client.auth.session(from:)` in DeepLink.swift; Android `exchangeCodeForSession` in DeepLinkRouter.kt), then a brand-styled set-password UI appears (NewPasswordSheet.swift / NewPasswordDialog.kt) — the user ends up signed in with the new password, no web hop. Cross-device caveat handled: PKCE verifier lives on the requesting device, so a link opened on a different device falls back to the branded web page — iOS via SFSafariViewController sheet, Android via an explicit default-browser intent (`Intent.makeMainSelectorActivity(ACTION_MAIN, CATEGORY_APP_BROWSER)` — a plain VIEW intent would loop back into the app link). (2) Email-confirmation links: signUp on all 3 platforms now passes emailRedirectTo/redirectUrl → wordocious.com/auth/confirm; new branded web /auth/confirm page exchanges the code; natives claim the path and confirm in-app when possible. Both restored by operator INSERT.
